| Database | Collection of files; data remains in an organized form |
| Data Capture | Process of collecting or capturing data from a site or a source |
| Data Manipulation | Captured data are manipulated to produce information |
| Data processing | Process to get meaningful information from data |
| DBMS | Database Management System; software package to manage database |
| DHTML | Dynamic Hyper Text Markup Language; creates dynamic content on web pages |
| Dial Up Networking | Method by which a computer is connected to the Internet using telephone |
| DOS | Disk Operating System; an operating system |
| Downloading | Transferring files from internet to computer memory |
| Ethics | Rules and beliefs |
| Fields | A field in database = collection of bytes containing data about an item |
| File | Collection of related records |
| GML | General Mark-up Language |
| Hacker | Person who breaks into a computer system to get access to the information stored there |
| HTML | Hyper Text Markup Language; used to create web-pages |
| IAB | Internet Activities Board, **formed 1983**; now called **Internet Architectural Board** |

### A.14 SOME MEMORABLE FACTS (p.495)
- Computer = **data processing machine**; two parts **hardware and software**; hardware = physical units; software = set of programs; both together make a system functional. **Data** = raw facts and figures.
- **Operating system** = interface between user and hardware; manages computer resources; performs different functions; responsible for process management, file management etc.; popular: **DOS, UNIX, Windows, LINUX, Mac OS**.
- **Windows Explorer** — divided vertically into **two panes**: **left pane** = disk drives and folders in hierarchical order; **right pane** = contents of the folder/drive selected on the left.
- Linking text values in a series within a formula = **'concatenation'**.
- Data processing involves data computing, data manipulation and information management.
- **Database** = collection of interrelated data. **Relational database** = data items organised as a set of formally described tables.
- **MS Access** = program to create and manage databases; **table** = collection of data about a specific topic; **form** = graphical representation of a table; **report** = presentation of data in printed format; can create mailing labels.
- **Internet** = network of computer networks; **originally developed by the US Department of Defence**.
- **World Wide Web invented by Tim Berners-Lee**. **FAT 32 and NTFS** = two major file systems in Microsoft Windows.
- **Websites** = files in servers; contain **Web Pages**; collection of all websites = **WWW**.
- **Hyper text** first coined by **Ted Nelson in 1960s**.
- **HTML** = markup language to create Web Pages using **Tags**; text editors used to compose HTML; viewed in Web browsers.
- Photoshop toolbox: tools grouped by type; small black triangle = more tools available on pop-up menu.
- **Most Popular Mobile OS = Android**, followed by **iOS & Windows**.
- **Versions of Android (old → new):** Cupcake → Donut → Eclairs → Froyo → Ginger Bread → Honeycomb → Ice Cream Sandwich → Jelly Beans → Kit-kat → Lollipop → Marshmallow → Nougat → Oreo → Pie → Android 10 → Android 11 (as printed).
- **Coding** = process of writing out computer instructions.
- **Four major digital initiatives to push e-education by the govt.: Swayam, Swayam Prabha, National Academic Depository, National Digital Library.**
- **Oracle** = database software.
- Speed of computer (clock speed) measured in **Hertz (Hz)**; modern processors in **Gigahertz (GHz)**; speed of communication over a data channel in **'Baud'**.
- **DHCP (Dynamic Host Configuration Protocol)** = network management protocol on TCP/IP networks for automatic, central management of IP addresses.
- A social network can be represented as a **graph**. **IPv6 defines an IP address of 128 bit**. Junk email = **'spam'**.
- Box: **Most popular Social Networking Sites — Facebook, Twitter, Linkedin and Instagram** (as printed). **Most popular online Shopping Websites — Amazon.com, Flipkart.com and ebay.com**.

### A.15 SOME MORE FACTS (p.496, before the Government-initiatives section)
- **Cloud Computing** — on-demand delivery of computer power, database storage, applications and other IT resources through a cloud services platform mainly via the internet; a distributed computer architecture providing software, infrastructure and platforms as required.
- **Spreadsheet / MS Excel** — numerical and statistical calculations. **CAD/CAM systems** — based on Interactive Computer Graphics.
- **Data Encryption Standard (DES)** — implementation of a **Feistel Cipher**; **16 round** Feistel structure; **block size 64 bits**.
- **Orthographic 3D Projection** (a form of parallel projection) — represents 3D objects in 2D.
- **Banker algorithm** (also called detection algorithm) — resource allocation and deadlock avoidance algorithm developed by **Edsger W. Dijkstra** (printed "Edsger").
- **Gateway** — connects two different networks using different protocols. **Half byte = 'Nibble'** (4 bits).
- **MS Excel versions 8.0–11.0** — **65,536 rows and 256 columns**; **version 12.0** — **10,48,576 rows and 16,384 columns**.
- **Robot 'Pepe'** — recently used by pupils at a primary school in Kerala; UK–India collaboration; encourages children to wash hands (as printed).
- India's first indigenously developed payment ecosystem for transport, based on **National Common Mobility Card (NCMC)** standards — launched **4 March 2019**.
- **Mobile App 'cVIGIL'** — launched by **Election Commission of India**; citizens report **Model Code of Conduct and Expenditure violations** during elections.
- **VISHWAS** — Video Integration and State Wide Advanced Security; first of its kind project in the country; e-governance initiative — law and order management, proactive traffic management, crime detection through video analytics, post-incident investigation and video forensics.
- **Gutter margin** — extra space added to side/top/inside margins of a document you plan to bind.
- **TAG Heuer** (premium watchmaker) associated with **Google** to develop a smart luxury watch.
- **Integrated circuit (IC)** — small chip functioning as amplifier, oscillator, timer, microprocessor, or computer memory; usually made of **silicon**; holds hundreds to millions of transistors, resistors and capacitors.
- **World Wide Web** — first Web browser developed in the form of hyperlinked applications mid/late 1980s; **Tim Berners-Lee** credited with developing in **1990** both the first web server and the first web browser, called **WorldWideWeb** (without spaces), later renamed **Nexus**.
- **Windows 10** released as a preview on **1 October 2014**; **Windows 11** released on **5 October 2021**.

### A.16 MISCELLANEOUS (pp.497–498, end of Chapter 12)
- **India's first Chat GPT-like service = 'Bharat GPT'.** BharatGPT group (led by **IIT Bombay** with **seven other elite tech institutes**) built the **'Hanooman' series** of Indian-language models, backed by **Reliance Industries Ltd** and **Department of Science and Technology (DST)** (⚠ recent item as printed).
- **GAI (Generative AI)** — branch of Artificial Intelligence emphasising generating new content (images, audio, text etc.) based on patterns learned from data.
- **Genie** [printed "Gienie"] — **Generative Interactive Environments** — foundation model trained on videos sourced from internet (⚠ recent item as printed).
- **QUBIT** = basic unit of Quantum information.
- **Cloud computing** — delivery of computing services (servers, storage, databases, networking, software, analytics, intelligence) over the Internet ("the cloud") for faster innovation, flexible resources, economies of scale.
- Fax uses **analogue signal**; email uses **digital signal**.
- **Blockchain** (per **IBM**) — shared, immutable ledger recording transactions and tracking assets in a business network; asset can be tangible (house, car, cash, land) or intangible (intellectual property, patents, copyrights, branding).
- **Virtual keyboards** protect against **Keylogger programs, password theft, spywares, Trojan programs**.
- E-mail that appears to originate from one source but is actually from another = **Phishing**.
- **Bus, Star and Grid** = common network topologies "while Grid is not a common network topology" (as printed; contradictory wording ⚠ — the sentence lists Grid both among and outside the common topologies; verify).
- **Router** = transfers data packets between two computer networks.
- Database containing names and addresses of various hosts on internet = **Domain Name System**.
- **SWIFT** founded **1973** by **239 banks in 15 countries** to create a unified international transaction processing and transmission system; in **1977** "prince of Belgium" sent first SWIFT message (as printed).
- The **average function** is listed in the drop-down menu of **Auto Sum** function tools.
- Emoticons are found at the bottom of the chat window in an instant-messaging conversation window.
- **Kerala** has the **first 100% fully digital literate Panchayat in India** (as printed).
- In MS Word tables, besides arrow keys and mouse clicks: use the **Tab key** or **double-clicking on the cell** to move around.
- In MS Word, the **floppy disk icon** below the Menu Bar = save current document.
- **Slide number** can be added in **Footer** of each slide of MS PowerPoint.
- In Microsoft Office, **font size** is determined by **Points**.
- MS Word **Different Odd & Even pages** feature — different headers and footers for odd and even pages.
- **GIS (Geographic Information System)** technology — most frequently used to manage and digitise **e-land records**.
- **Vision** is the main sense organ that **virtual reality** displays aim for.
- **.docx** occupies less memory when saving an MS-Word file.
- **Windows operating system** = example of **system software**.
- Web portal launched by **Department of Telecommunication** sharing info on **mobile towers and EMF Emission Compliances** = **Tarang Sanchar**.
- MS Word **Insert Column to the Right** — inserts a new column in a table while shifting existing columns to the right.
- The **'Date within'** field is clicked to choose the date for sorting in **Gmail**.
- (Page 499 = blank; chapter ends p.498.)

# STATIC GK — CENSUS: RAW EXTRACTION
(Project: Testbook Notes · SSC CGL Tier-I 2026 · Round 2)

**Source:** Parmar SSC Fatman (2nd Ed., 2026), Static GK Chapter 5 "CENSUS" (book pp.474–476, PDF 507–509). Lucent's GK (2026) has no separate Census chapter in the sections extracted for Static GK.
**NOTE:** Census is **not in the user's calendar list** for Static GK — extracted as an extra because it sits in Parmar's Static GK chapter list.
**Method:** page images read directly; the two religion tables (small, low-quality pictures) were re-rendered at higher resolution and read separately. Fact-complete extraction (every number, state/UT/district name, comparison chain kept); not word-for-word.
**Conventions:** ⚠ = doubt / printing slip / internal inconsistency / recent-event item. Chains like "A > B > C" are the book's ordering as printed (highest first). No test cross-referencing done.
**Cross-reference (do not re-extract):** Census/population geography items in `claude/geography-raw/human_geography_raw_extraction.md`.

---

## 1. CENSUS — BASICS AND HISTORY (p.474)
- Census = **official enumeration of the population** conducted periodically by the government.
- **Frequency:** once in every **10 years** in India.
- **Conducting authority:** **Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India**, under the **Ministry of Home Affairs (MoHA)**.
- **Legal framework: Census Act, 1948**.

**History of Census in India**
- First city where Census was conducted: **Dacca (now Dhaka, Bangladesh) in 1830 by Henry Walter** (also known as **"Father of Indian Census"**).
- **First Census (Non-Synchronized/Incomplete): 1872** (during **Lord Mayo's** tenure 1869–1872).
- **First Synchronized/Complete Census: 1881**, by the then Census Commissioner of India **W. C. Plowden** (during **Lord Ripon's** tenure 1880–1884).
- **First post-Independence Census: 1951**.
- **Latest Census conducted: 2011** (**15th Census** from 1872 and **7th post-Independence Census**).
- **Census 2021 was not conducted due to COVID-19 and "has been delayed till October 2024"** (⚠ as printed — recent-event item; the printed date is now in the past (today is 24 Sep 2026); check the current status from official sources).

## 2. CENSUS 2011 (p.474)
- **Registrar General of Census 2011: C. M. Chandramouli** (at present: **M. K. Narayan** as of April 2025 — ⚠ recent item as printed; the name is as read from the scan, verify).
- **Motto: "Our Census, Our Future"**.
- Data collected from **35 States/UTs and 640 Districts**.
- **Conducted in two phases:** House Listing (**April–September 2010**); Date of Enumeration **9–28 February 2011**, during which **29 questions** were asked.
- **Box:** Concept of **Standard Urban Area (SUA)** introduced during **1971 Census**.
- **Box:** **Maharashtra** has the **highest slum population**; **Delhi** has the highest slum population among UTs.

**India's population in 2011 Census**
- Total: **1.21 Billion (1210.2 M)**. India's share in world population **17.5%**; China's share **19.4%**.
- Male: **623.7 M (51.54%)**; Female: **586.46 M (48.46%)**.

## 3. HIGHEST AND LOWEST POPULATION (p.474)
- Box "According to area wise": **Highest: Andaman & Nicobar; Lowest: Lakshadweep** (as printed — context unclear; probably area).

| Category | Highest | Lowest |
|---|---|---|
| State | Uttar Pradesh (16% of total population) > Maharashtra > Bihar | Sikkim > Mizoram > Arunachal (printed "Sikkim Mizoram > Arunachal") |
| UT | Delhi | Lakshadweep (State + UT) |
| District | Thane (Maharashtra) | Dibang Valley (Arunachal Pradesh) |
| City | Mumbai | Kapurthala (Punjab) |

- Box: **Highest crude death rate: 1901–1921**.

## 4. GROWTH RATE (p.474)
- **Average annual growth rate (2001–2011): 1.64%**.
- **Decadal growth rate (2001–2011): 17.64%** (total increase **181 M (18.19 Cr)** people) → **Male 17.19%, Female 18.12%**.
- **1901 Census: 238 M**. **1951 Census: 361 M**.
- **Highest decadal growth rate (1961–1971): 24.8%** (⚠ as printed).
- **Lowest decadal growth rate (1911–1921): −0.31%** (reason: **famine**) (as printed).
- Box **"Year of the Great Divide (1921)" — Reason:** before 1921 high death rate, unstable population; after 1921 declining death rate, rising population.

**Decadal growth 2001–2011 (table)**
| Category | Highest | Lowest |
|---|---|---|
| State | Meghalaya (27.8%) > Arunachal Pradesh > Bihar | Nagaland (−0.47%) > Kerala > Goa |
| UT | Dadra & Nagar Haveli (55.5%) | Lakshadweep (6.23%) |
| District | Kurung Kumey (Arunachal Pradesh; 111%) | Longleng (Nagaland) |

## 5. CHILD POPULATION (0–6 years) (p.474)
- Total **158.8 M (13.1% of the population)** → **0.38% decreased** compared to 2001 (as printed).
- Highest: **Uttar Pradesh**; lowest: **Lakshadweep**.
- District-wise: **Kupwara, J & K (highest)** and **Dibang Valley, Arunachal Pradesh (lowest)**.

## 6. SEX RATIO (females per 1000 males) (pp.474–475)
- Sex ratio = number of females per 1000 males in a given area at a specified time.
- **2011 Census: 943; 2001 Census: 933**.

| Category | Highest | Lowest |
|---|---|---|
| State | Kerala (1084) > Tamil Nadu (996) > Andhra | Haryana (879) > J & K (889) |
| UT | Puducherry (1037), Lakshadweep (900) | Daman & Diu (618) > Dadra & Nagar Haveli (774) |
| District | Mahe (Puducherry) | Daman |

(⚠ As printed the "Highest" cell for UT lists Lakshadweep (900) after Puducherry (1037); the "Lowest" state chain "Haryana (879) > J & K (889)" — kept exactly as printed.)

**Child Sex Ratio (0–6 years)**
- **2001 Census: 927; 2011 Census: 919.** Box: "According Provisional Census: **914**".

| Category | Highest | Lowest |
|---|---|---|
| State | Arunachal Pradesh (972) > Mizoram | Haryana (834) |
| UT | Andaman & Nicobar (968) | Daman & Diu |
| District | Lahaul and Spiti Valley (Himachal Pradesh) | Jhajjar (Haryana) |

- Box: "As per Census 2011, **Maharashtra recorded negative growth in child sex ratio**" (as printed).

## 7. LITERACY RATE (p.475)
- In India, **only people aged 7 and above** are considered for literacy rates; literate = can read and write with understanding in any language. Box: **Adult (above 15 yrs) literacy: 69.3%**.
- **Overall:** 2001 Census **64.83%** → Male **75.85%**, Female **54.16%**; 2011 Census **74.04%** → Male **82.14%**, Female **65.46%**; **increase in literacy rate: 9.2%**.
- **Provisional Census 2011:** literacy **72.98%** → Male **80.9%**, Female **64.63%**. Box: **Provisional** = preliminary or tentative data subject to revision.
- **Difference in male and female literacy rate: 16.68%.**
- State with **highest difference** in male–female literacy: **Rajasthan (27.85%)**, 2nd **Jharkhand**; **least difference**: **Meghalaya**, 2nd **Kerala**.
- During last decade highest improvement in literacy rate: **Rural Females**.

| Category | Highest | Lowest |
|---|---|---|
| State | Kerala (94%) > Mizoram (91.33%) | Bihar (61.8%), Arunachal Pradesh (65.38%) |
| UT | Lakshadweep (91.85%) | Dadra & Nagar Haveli (77.24%) |
| District | Serchhip (Mizoram) | Alirajpur (Madhya Pradesh) |

**Literacy gender-wise:** Male (highest) **Kerala**; Female (highest) **Lakshadweep/Kerala**; Male (lowest) **Bihar**; Female (lowest) **Bihar** (as printed).
**Literacy category-wise (2011):** **Scheduled Caste 66.1%**; **Scheduled Tribe 59%**.

## 8. POPULATION DENSITY (p.475)
- Number of individuals per sq km. **2001: 325; 2011: 382.**

| Category | Highest | Lowest |
|---|---|---|
| State | Bihar (1106) > West Bengal > Kerala | Arunachal Pradesh (17) > Mizoram (52) |
| UT | Delhi (11,320) > Chandigarh | Andaman & Nicobar (46) |
| District | NE Delhi | Dibang Valley (Arunachal Pradesh) |

## 9. RURAL AND URBAN DIVIDE (p.475)
- **Rural: 833.1 M (68.8%)** — top 3 states **Uttar Pradesh > Bihar > West Bengal**; bottom 3 **Sikkim > Mizoram > Goa**.
- **Urban: 377.1 Million (31.2%)** — top 3 states **Maharashtra > Uttar Pradesh > Tamil Nadu**; bottom 3 **Sikkim > Arunachal Pradesh > Mizoram**.
- Box: **Highest % of rural population: Dadra & Nagar Haveli (among UTs)**; **Highest % of urban population: Delhi**.
- **Percentage-wise:** Rural (highest): **Himachal Pradesh (89.9%) > Bihar (88.7) > Assam (85.92%)**; Urban (highest): **Goa (62.17%) > Tamil Nadu (48.4%) > Kerala (47.7%)**.
- (⚠ Printed: "Rural 833.1 M (68.8%)" and "Urban 377.1 M (31.2%)"; the two add to 1210.2 M ✓; percentages as printed.)

## 10. CASTE-BASED POPULATION (pp.475–476)
- **Scheduled Caste (SC) population: 201 Million (16.6% of total population).** Highest population: **Uttar Pradesh**; highest percentage-wise: **Punjab**; lowest population: **Mizoram**; lowest percentage-wise: **Mizoram**; **States/UTs with 0% SC population: Nagaland, Arunachal Pradesh, Andaman & Nicobar, Lakshadweep** (as printed; ⚠ Mizoram is listed as lowest, and Nagaland/Arunachal as zero — internal wording as printed).
- **Scheduled Tribe (ST) population: 104.3 Million (8.6% of total population).** Highest population: **Madhya Pradesh**; highest percentage-wise: **Lakshadweep**; lowest population: **Haryana > Punjab > Delhi > Puducherry > Chandigarh** ("these three states have zero ST population" as printed).

- Largest **Buddhist and Jain** population: **Maharashtra**. Largest **Christian**: **Kerala**. Largest **Hindu**: **UP**. Largest **Muslim**: **J&K** (⚠ as printed; verify — the printed statement is for the state with the largest Muslim population). Highest % of **Hindu** population: **Himachal**. Highest % of **Muslim** population: **Lakshadweep (96.58%)**.

## 12. DEMOGRAPHIC DIVIDEND, WORKFORCE (p.476)
- **Demographic dividend** = period of economic growth when a country's population ages and the **working-age population increases**. **Working age group in India: 15–64 or 15–59** (as printed).
- **Work force in India:** Male work force participation **53.26%**; Female **25.5%**.
- **Himachal** has the highest **female LFPR (Labour Force Participation Rate) in rural India (47.4%)**; **Tamil Nadu** has the highest female LFPR in **urban** India.

## 13. MIGRATION TRENDS (p.476)
- Highest out-migration states: **Uttar Pradesh > Bihar**. Highest in-migration state: **Maharashtra**.
- Percentage of total migrants **from Nepal 15.1%**; **from Sri Lanka 2.9%** (as printed).
- **Main reason for migration:** men — **work and employment**; women — **marriage**.
- Box: **1st Socio-Economic and Caste Census (SECC) was first conducted in 1931 by Ministry of Rural Development** (as printed ⚠ — verify); **2.21% of India's population (26.8 Million) identified as disabled** (2011 Census); **Adolescent population: 21% (10–19 yrs)**.

- (Box headed "CENSUS 2011" as printed, but the values are 1951 Census data.) **Population according to 1951 Census: 361 M**; **Literacy rate 18.33% → Male 27.16%, Female 5.56%**; **Sex ratio 946**; **Population density 117.**
